you right there. Most features can actually be added via [GitHub Apps](https://docs.github.com/en/developers/apps), which extend GitHub and can be installed directly on organizations and user accounts and granted access to specific repositories. They come with granular permissions and built-in webhooks. Apps are first class actors within GitHub.\n\n## How it works\n\n**Probot is a framework for building [GitHub Apps](https://docs.github.com/en/developers/apps) in [Node.js](https://nodejs.org/)**, written in [TypeScript](https://www.typescriptlang.org/). GitHub Apps can listen to webhook events sent by a repository or organization. Probot uses its internal event emitter to perform actions based on those events. A simple Probot App might look like this:\n\n```js\nexport default (app) => {\n  app.on(\"issues.opened\", async (context) => {\n    const issueComment = context.issue({\n      body: \"Thanks for opening this issue!\",\n    });\n    return context.octokit.issues.createComment(issueComment);\n  });\n\n  app.onAny(async (context) => {\n    context.log.info({ event: context.name, action: context.payload.action });\n  });\n\n  app.onError(async (error) => {\n    app.log.error(error);\n  });\n};\n```\n\n## Building a Probot App\n\nIf you've landed in this GitHub repository and are looking to start building your own Probot App, look no further than [probot.github.io](https://probot.github.io/docs/)! The Probot website contains our extensive getting started documentation and will guide you through the set up process.\n\nThis repository hosts the code for the npm Probot package which is what all Probot Apps run on. Most folks who land in this repository are likely looking to get started [building their own app](https://probot.github.io/docs/).\n\n## Contributing\n\nProbot is built by people just like you! Most of the interesting things are built _with_ Probot, so consider starting by [writing a new app](https://probot.github.io/docs/) or improving one of the [existing ones](https://github.com/search?q=topic%3Aprobot-app&type=Repositories).\n\nIf you're interested in contributing to Probot itself, check out our [contributing docs](CONTRIBUTING.md) to get started.\n\nWant to discuss with Probot users and contributors? you right there. Most features can actually be added via [GitHub Apps](https://docs.github.com/en/developers/apps), which extend GitHub and can be installed directly on organizations and user accounts and granted access to specific repositories. They come with granular permissions and built-in webhooks. Apps are first class actors within GitHub.\n\n## How it works\n\n**Probot is a framework for building [GitHub Apps](https://docs.github.com/en/developers/apps) in [Node.js](https://nodejs.org/)**, written in [TypeScript](https://www.typescriptlang.org/). GitHub Apps can listen to webhook events sent by a repository or organization. Probot uses its internal event emitter to perform actions based on those events. A simple Probot App might look like this:\n\n```js\nexport default (app) => {\n  app.on(\"issues.opened\", async (context) => {\n    const issueComment = context.issue({\n      body: \"Thanks for opening this issue!\",\n    });\n    return context.octokit.issues.createComment(issueComment);\n  });\n\n  app.onAny(async (context) => {\n    context.log.info({ event: context.name, action: context.payload.action });\n  });\n\n  app.onError(async (error) => {\n    app.log.error(error);\n  });\n};\n```\n\n## Building a Probot App\n\nIf you've landed in this GitHub repository and are looking to start building your own Probot App, look no further than [probot.github.io](https://probot.github.io/docs/)! The Probot website contains our extensive getting started documentation and will guide you through the set up process.\n\nThis repository hosts the code for the npm Probot package which is what all Probot Apps run on. Most folks who land in this repository are likely looking to get started [building their own app](https://probot.github.io/docs/).\n\n## Contributing\n\nProbot is built by people just like you! Most of the interesting things are built _with_ Probot, so consider starting by [writing a new app](https://probot.github.io/docs/) or improving one of the [existing ones](https://github.com/search?q=topic%3Aprobot-app&type=Repositories).\n\nIf you're interested in contributing to Probot itself, check out our [contributing docs](CONTRIBUTING.md) to get started.\n\nWant to discuss with Probot users and contributors? [Discuss on GitHub](https://github.com/probot/probot/discussions)!\n\n## Ideas\n\nHave an idea for a cool new GitHub App (built with Probot)? That's great! Here’s a consolidated guide to what has changed, why, and what you need to update:\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Key Breaking Changes (from Probot v10 to later versions)\\n\\n### 1. **Deprecation of `context.github`**\\n\\n- In Probot v10, many examples and plugins used `context.github` as the handle to GitHub’s API.\\n- **In Probot v11 and up,** `context.github` has been **removed** in favor of `context.octokit`.\\n\\n#### **How to upgrade:**\\n\\n- **Replace** all uses of `context.github` with `context.octokit` in your code.\\n    ```js\\n    // Old (v10 and below)\\n    context.github.issues.createComment({...}); \\n    \\n    // New (v11+)\\n    context.octokit.issues.createComment({...});\\n    ```\\n\\n---\\n\\n### 2. **Octokit Constructor Changes**\\n\\n- Before: You might have imported or constructed octokit with `@octokit/rest` or used `context.github`.\\n- Now: The Octokit version used is upgraded and provided as `context.octokit` **with all the right authentication and plugins applied by Probot**.\\n\\n**You typically don’t need to manually construct or authenticate Octokit anymore:**  \\nJust use `context.octokit`.\\n\\n---\\n\\n### 3. **Authorized Functions**\\n\\n- Before: `context.github` sometimes exposed additional methods for making authenticated API calls.\\n- Now: `context.octokit` is your one-stop authenticated API client, already scoped for the right installation/user.\\n\\n---\\n\\n### 4. **Types and API Responses**\\n\\n- The Octokit response shapes may have changed as well as typings if you are using TypeScript.\\n- Make sure to check the Octokit documentation for **updated method signatures and return types** if you run into type errors after migration.\\n\\n---\\n\\n### 5. **Probot Plugin APIs**\\n\\n- Probot's APIs and certain hooks might have changed.
